# API field assertions — Postman-style checks on every journey request

Generated by `prototype/scripts/build-api-assertions.ts` from the responses real API journeys captured. Each case is one assertion the journey runner evaluates **live on every run** (`prototype/src/checks/apiAssertions.ts`) — the report shows it under the step as passed / failed / not checked. Required = present in every captured response; a format is claimed only when the field name names it and every observed value conforms. Do not edit by hand — re-run the script.

_Built 2026-09-29T11:55:55.875Z from 392 captured responses across 28 run(s)._

## 1. Sign in as the buyer — `GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`

Step `buyer_login` (buyer) · Postman: auth › generate otp, verify otp · seen in: RFQ only, Technical / RFP only, Technical + RFQ, Technical + Auction · 28 sample(s).

#### API-FBLOGIN-001 — Sign in as the buyer succeeds
- **Why it matters.** Every later check reads a success body; a refusal here is the headline, not a field detail.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical / RFP only, Technical + RFQ, Technical + Auction), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Note how the app answers.
- **Expected.** The app accepts the request and answers with a success.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `status_2xx`, learned from 28 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P0 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FBLOGIN-002 — Sign in as the buyer answers within 12 seconds
- **Why it matters.** A regression that makes a core call several times slower is a defect even when the body is right.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical / RFP only, Technical + RFQ, Technical + Auction), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Time the answer.
- **Expected.** The app answers within 12 seconds.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `max_ms 12000`, learned from 28 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P2 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

## 22. Score the vendor's technical response — `POST /evaluations`

Step `eval_score` (evaluator) · Postman: evaluation › create evaluations · seen in: Technical / RFP only, Technical + RFQ, Technical + Auction · 10 sample(s).

#### API-FESCORE-001 — Score the vendor's technical response succeeds
- **Why it matters.** Every later check reads a success body; a refusal here is the headline, not a field detail.

#### API-FAWDPROP-001 — Open an award proposal succeeds
- **Why it matters.** Every later check reads a success body; a refusal here is the headline, not a field detail.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ, Technical + Auction), at the step "Open an award proposal".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Open an award proposal” in the API journey (`POST /proposal`). 2. Note how the app answers.
- **Expected.** The app accepts the request and answers with a success.

- **Why it matters.** A regression that makes a core call several times slower is a defect even when the body is right.
